<issue_description>
Pf.build_pst fails when using longnames=False plus dataframe of pilot-points
From the discussions section,

When using a dataframe of predefined pilot-points with longnames=False in PstFrom, the template files are created fine but the build will either (1) fail completely if only pilot-points are in the setup, or (2) continue if the setup has parameters in addition to pilot-points but it won't swap the long names for short names in the pilot-point template files. 

From the error that is reported when the build fails completely (i.e., when you only have pilot-points) it looks like no entry is being made in the full_par_dat dataframe for the tpl_filename associated with the pilot-points when they are added via a dataframe. I guess this explains why the build continues without failing if parameters other than the pilot-points are included. 

You should be able to recreate the error by simply using longnames=False, adding pilot-points from a dataframe and then building Pst.

Hope that helps.
</issue_description>

- **Method affected:** `Pf.build_pst` 
- **Parameter causing issue:** `longnames=False`
- **Data input:** dataframe of predefined pilot-points
- **File type affected:** template files (.tpl)
- **Internal data structure:** `full_par_dat` dataframe
- **Missing field:** `tpl_filename` entries for pilot-points

**Problem Statement:**
When using `PstFrom` with `longnames=False` and adding pilot-points via a dataframe, the build process fails to properly register the template filenames in the internal `full_par_dat` dataframe. This causes:
1. Complete build failure when only pilot-points are present
2. Incomplete name swapping when other parameters are also present

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce
1. Create a `PstFrom` instance with `longnames=False`
2. Add pilot-points using a dataframe
3. Call `build_pst()` 
4. Observe failure or incorrect template file generation

### 1.5 Best Practices for Testing and Fixing
- Create minimal reproduction case
- Test both scenarios: pilot-points only vs pilot-points + other parameters
- Ensure fix doesn't break existing functionality
- Verify proper registration in `full_par_dat` dataframe
- Check that template files have correct short names substituted

     1	pyEMU
     2	=====
     3	
     4	python modules for model-independent FOSM (first-order, second-moment) (a.k.a linear-based, a.k.a. Bayes linear) 
     5	uncertainty analyses and data-worth analyses, non-linear uncertainty analyses and interfacing with PEST and PEST++.  
     6	pyEMU also has a pure python (pandas and numpy) implementation of ordinary kriging for geostatistical interpolation and 
     7	support for generating high-dimensional PEST(++) model interfaces, including support for (very) high-dimensional 
     8	ensemble generation and handling   
     9	
    10	Main branch:
    11	[![pyemu continuous integration](https://github.com/pypest/pyemu/actions/workflows/ci.yml/badge.svg?branch=main)](https://github.com/pypest/pyemu/actions/workflows/ci.yml)
    12	[![Coverage Status](https://coveralls.io/repos/github/pypest/pyemu/badge.svg?branch=main)](https://coveralls.io/github/pypest/pyemu?branch=main)

    16	[![Coverage Status](https://coveralls.io/repos/github/pypest/pyemu/badge.svg?branch=develop)](https://coveralls.io/github/pypest/pyemu?branch=develop)
    17	
    18	Documentation
    19	=============
    20	
    21	Complete user's guide:
    22	
    23	[https://pyemu.readthedocs.io/en/latest/](https://pyemu.readthedocs.io/en/latest/)
    24	
    25	The pyEMU documentation is being treated as a first-class citizen!  Also see the example notebooks in the repo.
    26	
    27	What is pyEMU?
    28	================
    29	
    30	pyEMU is a set of python modules for model-independent, user-friendly, computer model uncertainty analysis.  pyEMU is tightly coupled to the open-source suite PEST (Doherty 2010a and 2010b, and Doherty and other, 2010) and PEST++ (Welter and others, 2015, Welter and other, 2012), which are tools for model-independent parameter estimation.  However, pyEMU can be used with generic array objects, such as numpy ndarrays.
    31	
    32	Several equations are implemented, including Schur's complement for conditional uncertainty propagation (a.k.a. Bayes Linear estimation) (the foundation of the PREDUNC suite from PEST) and error variance analysis (the foundation of the PREDVAR suite of PEST).  pyEMU has easy-to-use routines for parameter and data worth analyses, which estimate how increased parameter knowledge and/or additional data effect forecast uncertainty in linear, Bayesian framework.  Support is also provided for high-dimensional Monte Carlo analyses via `ObservationEnsemble` and `ParameterEnsemble` class, including the null-space monte carlo approach of Tonkin and Doherty (2009); these ensemble classes also play nicely with PESTPP-IES.
    33	
    34	pyEMU also includes lots of functionality for dealing with PEST(++) datasets, such as:
    35	* manipulation of PEST control files, including the use of pandas for sophisticated editing of the parameter data and observation data sections
    36	* creation of PEST control files from instruction and template files
    37	* going between site sample files and pandas dataframes - really cool for observation processing
    38	* easy-to-use observation (re)weighting via residuals or user-defined functions
    39	* handling Jacobian and covariance matrices, including functionality to go between binary and ASCII matrices, reading and writing PEST uncertainty files.  Covariance matrices can be instantiated from relevant control file sections, such as parameter bounds or observation weights.  The base Matrix class overloads most common linear algebra operators so that operations are automatically aligned by row and column name.  Builtin SVD is also included in all Matrix instances.
    40	* geostatistics including geostatistical structure support, reading and writing PEST structure files and creating covariance matrices implied by nested geostatistical structures, and ordinary kriging (in the utils.geostats.OrdinaryKrige object), which replicates the functionality of pest utility ``ppk2fac``. 
    41	* composite scaled sensitivity calculations
    42	* calculation of correlation coefficient matrix from a given covariance matrix
    43	* Karhunen-Loeve-based parameterization as an alternative to pilot points for spatially-distributed parameter fields
    44	* a helper functions to start a group of tcp/ip workers on a local machine for parallel PEST++/BeoPEST runs
    45	* full support for prior information equations in control files
    46	* preferred differencing prior information equations where the weights are based on the Pearson correlation coefficient
    47	* verification-based tests based on results from several PEST utilities
    48	
    49	Version => 1.1 includes the `PstFrom` setup class to support generating PEST(++) interfaces in the 100,000 to 1,000,000 parameter range with all the bells and whistles.  A publication documenting the `PstFrom` class can be found here:
    50	
    51	[https://doi.org/10.1016/j.envsoft.2021.105022](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.envsoft.2021.105022)
    52	
    53	A publication documenting pyEMU and an example application can be found here:
    54	
    55	[http://dx.doi.org/10.1016/j.envsoft.2016.08.017](http://dx.doi.org/10.1016/j.envsoft.2016.08.017)
    56	
    57	
    58	Funding
    59	=======
    60	
    61	pyEMU was originally developed with support from the U.S. Geological Survey. The New Zealand Strategic Science Investment Fund as part of GNS Science’s (https://www.gns.cri.nz/) Groundwater Research Programme has also funded contributions 2018-present.  Intera, Inc. has also provided funding for pyEMU development and support
    62	
    63	Examples
    64	========
    65	
    66	Several example ipython notebooks are provided to demonstrate typical workflows for FOSM parameter and forecast uncertainty analysis as well as techniques to investigate parameter contributions to forecast uncertainty and observation data worth. Example models include the Henry saltwater intrusion problem (Henry 1964) and the model of Freyberg (1988)
    67	
    68	There is a whole world of detailed learning material for script-based approaches to parameter estimation and uncertainty quantification using PEST(++) at https://github.com/gmdsi/GMDSI_notebooks. These are and excellent resource for people picking up Pyemu for the first time and for those needing to revisit elements.
    69

    80	How to get started with pyEMU
    81	=============================
    82	
    83	pyEMU is available through pyPI and conda. To install pyEMU type:
    84	
    85	    >>>conda install -c conda-forge pyemu
    86	
    87	or
    88	
    89	    >>>pip install pyemu
    90	
    91	pyEMU needs `numpy` and `pandas`.  For plotting, `matplotloib`, `pyshp`, and `flopy` to take advantage of the auto interface construction
    92	
    93	After pyEMU is installed, the PEST++ software suite can be installed for your operating system  using the command:
    94	
    95	    get-pestpp :pyemu
    96	
    97	See [documentation](get_pestpp.md) for more information.
    98	
    99	Found a bug? Got a smart idea? Contributions welcome.
   100	====================================================
   101	Feel free to raise and issue or submit a pull request.
   102	
   103	pyEMU CI testing, using GitHub actions, has recently been switched over to run with [pytest](https://docs.pytest.org/).
   104	We make use of [pytest-xdist](https://pytest-xdist.readthedocs.io/en/latest/) for parallel execution. 
   105	Some notes that might be helpful for building your PR and testing:
   106	* Test files are in [./autotest](https://github.com/pypest/pyemu/tree/develop/autotest)
   107	* Pytest settings are in [./autotest/conftest.py](./autotest/conftest.py) and [./autotest/pytest.ini](./autotest/pytest.ini)
   108	* Currently, files ending `_tests.py` or `_tests_2.py` are collected
   109	* Functions starting `test_` or ending `_test` are collected
   110	* ipython notebooks in [.examples](./examples) are also run
   111	* As tests are run in parallel, where tests require read/write access to files it is safest to sandbox runs. 
   112	Pytest has a built-in fixture `tmp_path` that can help with this. 
   113	Setting optional argument `--basetemp` can be helpful for accessing the locally run files. 
   114	## Running test locally
   115	To be able to make clean use of pytests fixture decorators etc., 
   116	it is recommended to run local tests through `pytest` (rather than use from script execution and commenting in 
   117	__main__ block). For e.g.:
   118	### Run all tests: 
   119	> pytest --basetemp=runner autotest
   120	
   121	with pytest-xdist, local runs can be parallelized:
   122	> pytest --basetemp=runner -n auto autotest
   123	
   124	### Run all tests in a file: 
   125	> pytest --basetemp=runner -n auto autotest/testfile_tests.py
   126	
   127	### Run a specific test [`this_test()`]:
   128	> pytest --basetemp=runner autotest/testfile_tests.py::this_test
   129	
   130	### Using an IDE:
   131	Most modern, feature-rich editors and IDEs support launching pytest within debug or run consoles. 
   132	Some might need "encouraging" to recognise the non-standard test tags used in this library. 
   133	For example, in pycharm, to support click-and-run testing, the
   134	[pytest-imp](https://plugins.jetbrains.com/plugin/14202-pytest-imp) plugin is required to 
   135	pickup test functions that end with `_test` (a nosetest hangover in pyEMU).

    def copytree(src, dst, symlinks=False, ignore=None, copy_function=copy2,
                 ignore_dangling_symlinks=False, dirs_exist_ok=False):
        """Recursively copy a directory tree and return the destination directory.

        If exception(s) occur, an Error is raised with a list of reasons.

        If the optional symlinks flag is true, symbolic links in the
        source tree result in symbolic links in the destination tree; if
        it is false, the contents of the files pointed to by symbolic
        links are copied. If the file pointed by the symlink doesn't
        exist, an exception will be added in the list of errors raised in
        an Error exception at the end of the copy process.

        You can set the optional ignore_dangling_symlinks flag to true if you
        want to silence this exception. Notice that this has no effect on
        platforms that don't support os.symlink.

        The optional ignore argument is a callable. If given, it
        is called with the `src` parameter, which is the directory
        being visited by copytree(), and `names` which is the list of
        `src` contents, as returned by os.listdir():

            callable(src, names) -> ignored_names

        Since copytree() is called recursively, the callable will be
        called once for each directory that is copied. It returns a
        list of names relative to the `src` directory that should
        not be copied.

        The optional copy_function argument is a callable that will be used
        to copy each file. It will be called with the source path and the
        destination path as arguments. By default, copy2() is used, but any
        function that supports the same signature (like copy()) can be used.

        If dirs_exist_ok is false (the default) and `dst` already exists, a
        `FileExistsError` is raised. If `dirs_exist_ok` is true, the copying
        operation will continue if it encounters existing directories, and files
        within the `dst` tree will be overwritten by corresponding files from the
        `src` tree.
        """
        sys.audit("shutil.copytree", src, dst)

    def run(cmd_str, cwd=".", verbose=False):
        """an OS agnostic function to execute a command line

        Args:
            cmd_str (`str`): the str to execute with `os.system()`

            cwd (`str`, optional): the directory to execute the command in.
                Default is ".".
            verbose (`bool`, optional): flag to echo to stdout the  `cmd_str`.
                Default is `False`.

        Notes:
            uses `platform` to detect OS and adds .exe suffix or ./ prefix as appropriate
            if `os.system` returns non-zero, an exception is raised

        Example::

            pyemu.os_utils.run("pestpp-ies my.pst",cwd="template")

        """
        bwd = os.getcwd()
        os.chdir(cwd)
        try:
            exe_name = cmd_str.split()[0]
            if "window" in platform.platform().lower():
                if not exe_name.lower().endswith("exe"):
                    raw = cmd_str.split()
                    raw[0] = exe_name + ".exe"
                    cmd_str = " ".join(raw)
            else:
                if exe_name.lower().endswith("exe"):
                    raw = cmd_str.split()
                    exe_name = exe_name.replace(".exe", "")
                    raw[0] = exe_name
                    cmd_str = "{0} {1} ".format(*raw)
                if os.path.exists(exe_name) and not exe_name.startswith("./"):
                    cmd_str = "./" + cmd_str

        except Exception as e:
            os.chdir(bwd)
            raise Exception("run() error preprocessing command line :{0}".format(str(e)))
        if verbose:
            print("run():{0}".format(cmd_str))

        try:
            ret_val = os.system(cmd_str)
        except Exception as e:
            os.chdir(bwd)
            raise Exception("run() raised :{0}".format(str(e)))
        os.chdir(bwd)

        if "window" in platform.platform().lower():
            if ret_val != 0:
                raise Exception("run() returned non-zero: {0}".format(ret_val))
        else:
            estat = os.WEXITSTATUS(ret_val)
            if estat != 0 or ret_val != 0:
>               raise Exception("run() returned non-zero: {0},{1}".format(estat,ret_val))

        pest interface

        Args:
            filenames (`str`): Model input filenames to parameterize. By default filename should give relative
                loction from top level of pest template directory
                (`new_d` as passed to `PstFrom()`).
            par_type (`str`): One of `grid` - for every element, `constant` - for single
                parameter applied to every element, `zone` - for zone-based
                parameterization or `pilotpoint` - for
                pilot-point base parameterization of array style input files.
                Note `kl` not yet implemented # TODO

                for parameterization.

            lower_bound (`float`): PEST parameter lower bound.  If `None` and `transform` is "log", then 1.0e-10 is used.
                Otherwise, if `None`, -1.0e+10 is used.  Default is `None`
            transform (`str`): PEST parameter transformation.  Must be either "log","none" or "fixed.  The "tied" transform
                must be used after calling `PstFrom.build_pst()`.
--

   554	def pilot_points_to_tpl(pp_file, tpl_file=None, name_prefix=None):
   555	    """write a template file for a pilot points file
   556	
   557	    Args:
   558	        pp_file : (`str`): existing pilot points file
   559	        tpl_file (`str`): template file name to write.  If None,
   560	            `pp_file`+".tpl" is used.  Default is `None`.
   561	        name_prefix (`str`): name to prepend to parameter names for each
   562	            pilot point.  For example, if `name_prefix = "hk_"`, then each
   563	            pilot point parameters will be named "hk_0001","hk_0002", etc.
   564	            If None, parameter names from `pp_df.name` are used.
   565	            Default is None.
   566	
   567	    Returns:
   568	        `pandas.DataFrame`: a dataframe with pilot point information
   569	        (name,x,y,zone,parval1) with the parameter information
   570	        (parnme,tpl_str)
   571	
   572	    Example::
   573	
   574	        pyemu.pp_utils.pilot_points_to_tpl("my_pps.dat",name_prefix="my_pps")
   575	
   576	
   577	    """
   578	
   579	    if isinstance(pp_file, pd.DataFrame):
   580	        pp_df = pp_file
   581	        assert tpl_file is not None
   582	    else:
   583	        assert os.path.exists(pp_file)
   584	        pp_df = pd.read_csv(pp_file, sep=r"\s+",
   585	                            header=None, names=PP_NAMES)
   586	    pp_df = pp_df.astype({'zone': int}, errors='ignore')
   587	    if tpl_file is None:
   588	        tpl_file = pp_file + ".tpl"
   589	
   590	    if name_prefix is not None:
   591	        if "i" in pp_df.columns and "j" in pp_df.columns:
   592	            pp_df.loc[:, "parnme"] = pp_df.apply(
   593	                lambda x: "{0}_i:{1}_j:{2}".format(name_prefix, int(x.i), int(x.j)),
   594	                axis=1,
   595	            )
   596	        elif "x" in pp_df.columns and "y" in pp_df.columns:
   597	            pp_df.loc[:, "parnme"] = pp_df.apply(
   598	                lambda x: "{0}_x:{1:0.2f}_y:{2:0.2f}".format(name_prefix, x.x, x.y),
   599	                axis=1,
   600	            )
</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/pypest__pyemu__1.3/pyemu/utils/pp_utils.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>view</arg_value><arg_key>view_range</arg_key><arg_value>[600, 650]</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Here's the result of running `cat -n` on /workspace/pypest__pyemu__1.3/pyemu/utils/pp_utils.py:
   600	            )
   601	        else:
   602	            pp_df.loc[:, "idx"] = np.arange(pp_df.shape[0])
   603	            pp_df.loc[:, "parnme"] = pp_df.apply(
   604	                lambda x: "{0}_ppidx:{1}".format(name_prefix, x.idx),
   605	                axis=1,
   606	            )
   607	        if "zone" in pp_df.columns:
   608	            pp_df.loc[:, "parnme"] = pp_df.apply(
   609	                lambda x: x.parnme + "_zone:{0}".format(x.zone), axis=1
   610	            )
   611	        pp_df.loc[:, "tpl"] = pp_df.parnme.apply(
   612	            lambda x: "~    {0}    ~".format(x)
   613	        )
   614	    else:
   615	        pp_df.loc[:, "parnme"] = pp_df.name
   616	        pp_df.loc[:, "tpl"] = pp_df.parnme.apply(
   617	            lambda x: "~    {0}    ~".format(x)
   618	        )
   619	    _write_df_tpl(
   620	        tpl_file,
   621	        pp_df.loc[:, ["name", "x", "y", "zone", "tpl"]],
   622	        sep=" ",
   623	        index_label="index",
   624	        header=False,
   625	        index=False,
   626	        quotechar=" ",
   627	        quoting=2,
   628	    )
   629	
   630	    return pp_df

   632	def get_zoned_ppoints_for_vertexgrid(spacing, zone_array, mg, zone_number=None, add_buffer=True):

   634	 
   635	    Args:
   636	        spacing (`float`): spacing in model length units between pilot points.

"""
Script to reproduce the issue with Pf.build_pst failing when using longnames=False 
plus dataframe of pilot-points.
"""

import os
import tempfile
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import pyemu
from pathlib import Path

def create_simple_test_case():
    """Create a minimal test case to reproduce the issue."""
    
    # Create temporary directories
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
        temp_path = Path(temp_dir)
        original_d = temp_path / "original"
        template_d = temp_path / "template"
        
        original_d.mkdir()
        template_d.mkdir()
        
        # Create a simple dummy file to parameterize
        dummy_file = original_d / "dummy_param.txt"
        with open(dummy_file, 'w') as f:
            f.write("1.0\n2.0\n3.0\n")
            
        print(f"Created test directories: {temp_path}")
        print(f"Original dir: {original_d}")
        print(f"Template dir: {template_d}")
        
        # Set up PstFrom object with longnames=False
        pf = pyemu.utils.PstFrom(
            original_d=str(original_d),
            new_d=str(template_d),
            longnames=False,  # This is the key setting that causes the issue
            remove_existing=True
        )
        
        # Create a simple pilot point dataframe
        pp_data = {
            'name': ['pp1', 'pp2'],
            'x': [100.0, 200.0],
            'y': [100.0, 200.0],
            'zone': [1, 1],
            'parval1': [1.0, 1.0]
        }
        pp_df = pd.DataFrame(pp_data)
        print("Created pilot point dataframe:")
        print(pp_df)
        
        # Try to add pilot points using the dataframe
        try:
            print("\nAdding pilot points with longnames=False...")
            df = pf.add_parameters(
                filenames="dummy_param.txt",
                par_type="pilotpoint",
                pp_space=pp_df,  # Using dataframe of pilot points
                par_name_base="test_pp"
            )
            print("Successfully added pilot points")
            print(f"Pilot point dataframe shape: {df.shape}")
            print("Columns:", df.columns.tolist())
            
            # Check if tpl_filename column exists
            if 'tpl_filename' in df.columns:
                print("tpl_filename column found in pilot point dataframe")
                print("tpl_filename values:", df['tpl_filename'].tolist())
            else:
                print("WARNING: tpl_filename column NOT found in pilot point dataframe")
                
            # Try to build the PST
            print("\nBuilding PST...")
            pst = pf.build_pst("test.pst")
            print("Successfully built PST!")
            
            return True
            
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"ERROR occurred: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
            import traceback
            traceback.print_exc()
            return False

def create_test_case_with_regular_params():
    """Create a test case with both pilot points and regular parameters."""
    
    # Create temporary directories
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
        temp_path = Path(temp_dir)
        original_d = temp_path / "original"
        template_d = temp_path / "template"
        
        original_d.mkdir()
        template_d.mkdir()
        
        # Create dummy files to parameterize
        dummy_file1 = original_d / "dummy_param1.txt"
        with open(dummy_file1, 'w') as f:
            f.write("1.0\n2.0\n3.0\n")
            
        dummy_file2 = original_d / "dummy_param2.txt" 
        with open(dummy_file2, 'w') as f:
            f.write("4.0\n5.0\n6.0\n")
            
        print(f"Created test directories: {temp_path}")
        
        # Set up PstFrom object with longnames=False
        pf = pyemu.utils.PstFrom(
            original_d=str(original_d),
            new_d=str(template_d),
            longnames=False,  # This is the key setting that causes the issue
            remove_existing=True
        )
        
        # Create a simple pilot point dataframe
        pp_data = {
            'name': ['pp1', 'pp2'],
            'x': [100.0, 200.0],
            'y': [100.0, 200.0],
            'zone': [1, 1],
            'parval1': [1.0, 1.0]
        }
        pp_df = pd.DataFrame(pp_data)
        print("Created pilot point dataframe:")
        print(pp_df)
        
        try:
            # First add regular grid parameters
            print("\nAdding regular grid parameters...")
            df_grid = pf.add_parameters(
                filenames="dummy_param1.txt",
                par_type="grid",
                par_name_base="test_grid"
            )
            print("Successfully added grid parameters")
            
            # Then add pilot points using the dataframe
            print("\nAdding pilot points with longnames=False...")
            df_pp = pf.add_parameters(
                filenames="dummy_param2.txt",
                par_type="pilotpoint",
                pp_space=pp_df,  # Using dataframe of pilot points
                par_name_base="test_pp"
            )
            print("Successfully added pilot points")
            
            # Check if tpl_filename column exists in both dataframes
            print("\nChecking grid parameters dataframe:")
            if 'tpl_filename' in df_grid.columns:
                print("tpl_filename column found in grid dataframe")
            else:
                print("tpl_filename column NOT found in grid dataframe")
                
            print("Checking pilot point parameters dataframe:")
            if 'tpl_filename' in df_pp.columns:
                print("tpl_filename column found in pilot point dataframe")
            else:
                print("tpl_filename column NOT found in pilot point dataframe")
                
            # Check what's in par_dfs
            print(f"\nNumber of dataframes in par_dfs: {len(pf.par_dfs)}")
            for i, df in enumerate(pf.par_dfs):
                print(f"DataFrame {i} columns: {df.columns.tolist()}")
                if 'tpl_filename' in df.columns:
                    print(f"  tpl_filename values: {df['tpl_filename'].tolist()}")
                else:
                    print("  NO tpl_filename column!")
                    
            # Try to build the PST
            print("\nBuilding PST...")
            pst = pf.build_pst("test.pst")
            print("Successfully built PST!")
            
            # Check parameter names in final PST
            print(f"\nFinal PST parameter count: {pst.npar}")
            print("First few parameter names:")
            for i, parnme in enumerate(pst.parameter_data.index[:5]):
                print(f"  {i+1}: {parnme}")
                
            return True
            
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"ERROR occurred: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
            import traceback
            traceback.print_exc()
            return False

if __name__ == "__main__":

"""
Script to reproduce the issue with Pf.build_pst failing when using longnames=False 
plus dataframe of pilot-points.
"""

import os
import tempfile
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import pyemu
from pathlib import Path

def create_simple_test_case():
    """Create a minimal test case to reproduce the issue."""
    
    # Create temporary directories
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
        temp_path = Path(temp_dir)
        original_d = temp_path / "original"
        template_d = temp_path / "template"
        
        original_d.mkdir()
        template_d.mkdir()
        
        # Create a simple dummy file to parameterize
        dummy_file = original_d / "dummy_param.txt"
        with open(dummy_file, 'w') as f:
            f.write("1.0\n2.0\n3.0\n")
            
        print(f"Created test directories: {temp_path}")
        print(f"Original dir: {original_d}")
        print(f"Template dir: {template_d}")
        
        # Set up PstFrom object with longnames=False
        pf = pyemu.utils.PstFrom(
            original_d=str(original_d),
            new_d=str(template_d),
            longnames=False,  # This is the key setting that causes the issue
            remove_existing=True
        )
        
        # Create a simple pilot point dataframe
        pp_data = {
            'name': ['pp1', 'pp2'],
            'x': [100.0, 200.0],
            'y': [100.0, 200.0],
            'zone': [1, 1],
            'parval1': [1.0, 1.0]
        }
        pp_df = pd.DataFrame(pp_data)
        print("Created pilot point dataframe:")
        print(pp_df)
        
        # Try to add pilot points using the dataframe
        try:
            print("\nAdding pilot points with longnames=False...")
            df = pf.add_parameters(
                filenames="dummy_param.txt",
                par_type="pilotpoint",
                pp_space=pp_df,  # Using dataframe of pilot points
                par_name_base="test_pp"
            )
            print("Successfully added pilot points")
            print(f"Pilot point dataframe shape: {df.shape}")
            print("Columns:", df.columns.tolist())
            
            # Check if tpl_filename column exists
            if 'tpl_filename' in df.columns:
                print("tpl_filename column found in pilot point dataframe")
                print("tpl_filename values:", df['tpl_filename'].tolist())
            else:
                print("WARNING: tpl_filename column NOT found in pilot point dataframe")
                
            # Try to build the PST
            print("\nBuilding PST...")
            pst = pf.build_pst("test.pst")
            print("Successfully built PST!")
            
            return True
            
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"ERROR occurred: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
            import traceback
            traceback.print_exc()
            return False

def create_test_case_with_regular_params():
    """Create a test case with both pilot points and regular parameters."""
    
    # Create temporary directories
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
        temp_path = Path(temp_dir)
        original_d = temp_path / "original"
        template_d = temp_path / "template"
        
        original_d.mkdir()
        template_d.mkdir()
        
        # Create dummy files to parameterize
        dummy_file1 = original_d / "dummy_param1.txt"
        with open(dummy_file1, 'w') as f:
            f.write("1.0\n2.0\n3.0\n")
            
        dummy_file2 = original_d / "dummy_param2.txt" 
        with open(dummy_file2, 'w') as f:
            f.write("4.0\n5.0\n6.0\n")
            
        print(f"Created test directories: {temp_path}")
        
        # Set up PstFrom object with longnames=False
        pf = pyemu.utils.PstFrom(
            original_d=str(original_d),
            new_d=str(template_d),
            longnames=False,  # This is the key setting that causes the issue
            remove_existing=True
        )
        
        # Create a simple pilot point dataframe
        pp_data = {
            'name': ['pp1', 'pp2'],
            'x': [100.0, 200.0],
            'y': [100.0, 200.0],
            'zone': [1, 1],
            'parval1': [1.0, 1.0]
        }
        pp_df = pd.DataFrame(pp_data)
        print("Created pilot point dataframe:")
        print(pp_df)
        
        try:
            # First add regular grid parameters
            print("\nAdding regular grid parameters...")
            df_grid = pf.add_parameters(
                filenames="dummy_param1.txt",
                par_type="grid",
                par_name_base="test_grid"
            )
            print("Successfully added grid parameters")
            
            # Then add pilot points using the dataframe
            print("\nAdding pilot points with longnames=False...")
            df_pp = pf.add_parameters(
                filenames="dummy_param2.txt",
                par_type="pilotpoint",
                pp_space=pp_df,  # Using dataframe of pilot points
                par_name_base="test_pp"
            )
            print("Successfully added pilot points")
            
            # Check if tpl_filename column exists in both dataframes
            print("\nChecking grid parameters dataframe:")
            if 'tpl_filename' in df_grid.columns:
                print("tpl_filename column found in grid dataframe")
            else:
                print("tpl_filename column NOT found in grid dataframe")
                
            print("Checking pilot point parameters dataframe:")
            if 'tpl_filename' in df_pp.columns:
                print("tpl_filename column found in pilot point dataframe")
            else:
                print("tpl_filename column NOT found in pilot point dataframe")
                
            # Check what's in par_dfs
            print(f"\nNumber of dataframes in par_dfs: {len(pf.par_dfs)}")
            for i, df in enumerate(pf.par_dfs):
                print(f"DataFrame {i} columns: {df.columns.tolist()}")
                if 'tpl_filename' in df.columns:
                    print(f"  tpl_filename values: {df['tpl_filename'].tolist()}")
                else:
                    print("  NO tpl_filename column!")
                    
            # Try to build the PST
            print("\nBuilding PST...")
            pst = pf.build_pst("test.pst")
            print("Successfully built PST!")
            
            # Check parameter names in final PST
            print(f"\nFinal PST parameter count: {pst.npar}")
            print("First few parameter names:")
            for i, parnme in enumerate(pst.parameter_data.index[:5]):
                print(f"  {i+1}: {parnme}")
                
            return True
            
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"ERROR occurred: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
            import traceback
            traceback.print_exc()
            return False

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("=" * 60)

import os

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import pyemu
from pathlib import Path

def create_simple_test_case():
    """Create a minimal test case to reproduce the issue."""
    
    # Create temporary directories
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
        temp_path = Path(temp_dir)
        original_d = temp_path / "original"
        template_d = temp_path / "template"
        
        original_d.mkdir()
        template_d.mkdir()
        
        # Create a simple dummy file to parameterize with proper format
        dummy_file = original_d / "dummy_param.txt"
        with open(dummy_file, 'w') as f:
            f.write("1  1.2345678   1.2345678  1.2345678 9.87654321\n")
            f.write("2  2.2345678   2.2345678  2.2345678 8.87654321\n")
            
        print(f"Created test directories: {temp_path}")
        print(f"Original dir: {original_d}")
        print(f"Template dir: {template_d}")
        
        # Set up PstFrom object with longnames=False
        pf = pyemu.utils.PstFrom(
            original_d=str(original_d),
            new_d=str(template_d),
            longnames=False,  # This is the key setting that causes the issue
            remove_existing=True
        )
        
        # Create a simple pilot point dataframe
        pp_data = {
            'name': ['pp1', 'pp2'],
            'x': [100.0, 200.0],
            'y': [100.0, 200.0],
            'zone': [1, 1],
            'parval1': [1.0, 1.0]
        }
        pp_df = pd.DataFrame(pp_data)
        print("Created pilot point dataframe:")
        print(pp_df)
        
        # Try to add pilot points using the dataframe
        try:
            print("\nAdding pilot points with longnames=False...")
            df = pf.add_parameters(
                filenames="dummy_param.txt",
                par_type="pilotpoint",
                pp_space=pp_df,  # Using dataframe of pilot points
                par_name_base="test_pp"
            )
            print("Successfully added pilot points")
            print(f"Pilot point dataframe shape: {df.shape}")
            print("Columns:", df.columns.tolist())
            
            # Check if tpl_filename column exists
            if 'tpl_filename' in df.columns:
                print("tpl_filename column found in pilot point dataframe")
                print("tpl_filename values:", df['tpl_filename'].tolist())
            else:
                print("WARNING: tpl_filename column NOT found in pilot point dataframe")
                
            # Check what's in par_dfs
            print(f"\nNumber of dataframes in par_dfs: {len(pf.par_dfs)}")
            for i, df in enumerate(pf.par_dfs):
                print(f"DataFrame {i} columns: {df.columns.tolist()}")
                if 'tpl_filename' in df.columns:
                    print(f"  tpl_filename values: {df['tpl_filename'].tolist()}")
                else:
                    print("  NO tpl_filename column!")
                    
            # Try to build the PST
            print("\nBuilding PST...")
            pst = pf.build_pst("test.pst")

            print(f"ERROR occurred: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
            import traceback
            traceback.print_exc()
            return False

    16	    """Create a minimal test case to reproduce the issue."""
    17	    
    18	    # Create temporary directories
    19	    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
    20	        temp_path = Path(temp_dir)
    21	        original_d = temp_path / "original"
    22	        template_d = temp_path / "template"
    23	        
    24	        original_d.mkdir()
    25	        template_d.mkdir()
    26	        
    27	        # Create a simple dummy file to parameterize with proper format
    28	        dummy_file = original_d / "dummy_param.txt"
    29	        with open(dummy_file, 'w') as f:
    30	            f.write("1  1.2345678   1.2345678  1.2345678 9.87654321\n")
    31	            f.write("2  2.2345678   2.2345678  2.2345678 8.87654321\n")
    32	            
    33	        print(f"Created test directories: {temp_path}")
    34	        print(f"Original dir: {original_d}")
    35	        print(f"Template dir: {template_d}")
    36	        
    37	        # Set up PstFrom object with longnames=False
    38	        pf = pyemu.utils.PstFrom(
    39	            original_d=str(original_d),
    40	            new_d=str(template_d),
    41	            longnames=False,  # This is the key setting that causes the issue
    42	            remove_existing=True
    43	        )
    44	        
    45	        # Create a simple pilot point dataframe
    46	        pp_data = {
    47	            'name': ['pp1', 'pp2'],
    48	            'x': [100.0, 200.0],
    49	            'y': [100.0, 200.0],
    50	            'zone': [1, 1],
    51	            'parval1': [1.0, 1.0]
    52	        }
    53	        pp_df = pd.DataFrame(pp_data)
    54	        print("Created pilot point dataframe:")
    55	        print(pp_df)
    56	        
    57	        # Try to add pilot points using the dataframe
    58	        try:
    59	            print("\nAdding pilot points with longnames=False...")
    60	            df = pf.add_parameters(
    61	                filenames="dummy_param.txt",
    62	                par_type="pilotpoint",
    63	                pp_space=pp_df,  # Using dataframe of pilot points
    64	                par_name_base="test_pp"
    65	            )
    66	            print("Successfully added pilot points")
    67	            print(f"Pilot point dataframe shape: {df.shape}")
    68	            print("Columns:", df.columns.tolist())
    69	            
    70	            # Check if tpl_filename column exists
    71	            if 'tpl_filename' in df.columns:
    72	                print("tpl_filename column found in pilot point dataframe")
    73	                print("tpl_filename values:", df['tpl_filename'].tolist())
    74	            else:
    75	                print("WARNING: tpl_filename column NOT found in pilot point dataframe")
    76	                
    77	            # Check what's in par_dfs
    78	            print(f"\nNumber of dataframes in par_dfs: {len(pf.par_dfs)}")
    79	            for i, df in enumerate(pf.par_dfs):
    80	                print(f"DataFrame {i} columns: {df.columns.tolist()}")
    81	                if 'tpl_filename' in df.columns:
    82	                    print(f"  tpl_filename values: {df['tpl_filename'].tolist()}")
    83	                else:
    84	                    print("  NO tpl_filename column!")
    85	                    
    86	            # Try to build the PST
    87	            print("\nBuilding PST...")
    88	            pst = pf.build_pst("test.pst")
    89	            print("Successfully built PST!")
    90	            
    91	            return True
    92	            
    93	        except Exception as e:
    94	            print(f"ERROR occurred: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
    95	            import traceback
    96	            traceback.print_exc()
    97	            return False

"""
Script to reproduce the issue with Pf.build_pst failing when using longnames=False 
plus dataframe of pilot-points.
"""

import os
import tempfile
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import pyemu
from pathlib import Path

def create_simple_test_case():
    """Create a minimal test case to reproduce the issue."""
    
    # Create temporary directories
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
        temp_path = Path(temp_dir)
        original_d = temp_path / "original"
        template_d = temp_path / "template"
        
        original_d.mkdir()
        template_d.mkdir()
        
        # Create a simple dummy file to parameterize with proper format
        dummy_file = original_d / "dummy_param.txt"
        with open(dummy_file, 'w') as f:
            f.write("1  1.2345678   1.2345678  1.2345678 9.87654321\n")
            f.write("2  2.2345678   2.2345678  2.2345678 8.87654321\n")
            
        print(f"Created test directories: {temp_path}")
        print(f"Original dir: {original_d}")
        print(f"Template dir: {template_d}")
        
        # Set up PstFrom object with longnames=False
        pf = pyemu.utils.PstFrom(
            original_d=str(original_d),
            new_d=str(template_d),
            longnames=False,  # This is the key setting that causes the issue
            remove_existing=True
        )
        
        # Create a simple pilot point dataframe
        pp_data = {
            'name': ['pp1', 'pp2'],
            'x': [100.0, 200.0],
            'y': [100.0, 200.0],
            'zone': [1, 1],
            'parval1': [1.0, 1.0]
        }
        pp_df = pd.DataFrame(pp_data)
        print("Created pilot point dataframe:")
        print(pp_df)
        
        # Try to add pilot points using the dataframe
        try:
            print("\nAdding pilot points with longnames=False...")
            df = pf.add_parameters(
                filenames="dummy_param.txt",
                par_type="pilotpoint",
                pp_space=pp_df,  # Using dataframe of pilot points
                par_name_base="test_pp"
            )
            print("Successfully added pilot points")
            print(f"Pilot point dataframe shape: {df.shape}")
            print("Columns:", df.columns.tolist())
            
            # Check if tpl_filename column exists
            if 'tpl_filename' in df.columns:
                print("tpl_filename column found in pilot point dataframe")
                print("tpl_filename values:", df['tpl_filename'].tolist())
            else:
                print("WARNING: tpl_filename column NOT found in pilot point dataframe")
                
            # Check what's in par_dfs
            print(f"\nNumber of dataframes in par_dfs: {len(pf.par_dfs)}")
            for i, df in enumerate(pf.par_dfs):
                print(f"DataFrame {i} columns: {df.columns.tolist()}")
                if 'tpl_filename' in df.columns:
                    print(f"  tpl_filename values: {df['tpl_filename'].tolist()}")
                else:
                    print("  NO tpl_filename column!")
                    
            # Try to build the PST
            print("\nBuilding PST...")
            pst = pf.build_pst("test.pst")
            print("Successfully built PST!")
            
            return True
            
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"ERROR occurred: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
            import traceback
            traceback.print_exc()
            return False

"""
Simple script to reproduce the exact issue: 
tpl_filename column missing from pilot point dataframe when using longnames=False
"""

import os
import tempfile
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import pyemu
from pathlib import Path

def test_tpl_filename_missing():
    """Test that demonstrates the missing tpl_filename column issue."""
    
    print("Creating simple test case to show missing tpl_filename column...")
    
    # Create temporary directories
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
        temp_path = Path(temp_dir)
        original_d = temp_path / "original"
        template_d = temp_path / "template"
        
        original_d.mkdir()
        template_d.mkdir()
        
        # Create a simple dummy file to parameterize
        dummy_file = original_d / "param.dat"
        with open(dummy_file, 'w') as f:
            f.write("1  1.0  2.0  3.0\n")
            f.write("2  4.0  5.0  6.0\n")
            
        # Set up PstFrom object with longnames=False
        pf = pyemu.utils.PstFrom(
            original_d=str(original_d),
            new_d=str(template_d),
            longnames=False,  # This triggers the short name conversion logic
            remove_existing=True
        )
        
        # Create a simple pilot point dataframe
        pp_data = {
            'name': ['pp1', 'pp2'],
            'x': [100.0, 200.0],
            'y': [100.0, 200.0],
            'zone': [1, 1],
            'parval1': [1.0, 1.0]
        }
        pp_df = pd.DataFrame(pp_data)
        print("Created pilot point dataframe:")
        print(pp_df)
        
        # Mock the pilot_points_to_tpl function result to simulate what happens
        # inside add_parameters for pilot points
        pilot_df_result = pd.DataFrame({
            'parnme': ['test_pp1', 'test_pp2'],
            'name': ['pp1', 'pp2'],
            'x': [100.0, 200.0],
            'y': [100.0, 200.0],
            'zone': [1, 1],
            'parval1': [1.0, 1.0],
            'pargp': ['test_pp_pargp', 'test_pp_pargp']
        })
        
        # This simulates what happens in the pilot point processing code
        # around line 2584-2590 in pst_from.py
        pilot_df_result.loc[:, "pargp"] = ['test_pp_pargp']
        pilot_df_result.set_index("parnme", drop=False, inplace=True)
        
        print("\nPilot point dataframe after processing (before adding to par_dfs):")
        print("Columns:", pilot_df_result.columns.tolist())
        
        # Check if tpl_filename column exists
        if 'tpl_filename' in pilot_df_result.columns:
            print("✓ tpl_filename column FOUND")
        else:
            print("✗ tpl_filename column MISSING")
            print("This is the bug - tpl_filename should be set but it's commented out!")
            
        # Simulate what happens when we add to par_dfs and then try to build PST
        # This mimics the logic around lines 739-805 in build_pst()
        pf.par_dfs = [pilot_df_result]
        
        print(f"\nNumber of dataframes in par_dfs: {len(pf.par_dfs)}")
        for i, df in enumerate(pf.par_dfs):
            print(f"DataFrame {i} columns: {df.columns.tolist()}")
            if 'tpl_filename' in df.columns:
                print(f"  tpl_filename values: {df['tpl_filename'].tolist()}")
            else:
                print("  NO tpl_filename column!")
                
        # Now try to build the PST - this should fail when longnames=False
        # because the tpl_filename column is missing
        try:
            print("\nAttempting to build PST with longnames=False...")
            # This will trigger the short name conversion logic around line 790+
            # which tries to group by tpl_filename
            full_par_dat = pd.concat(pf.par_dfs)
            print("Full parameter data columns:", full_par_dat.columns.tolist())
            
            # This is the line that fails (around line 804):
            # tpl_shortlist = full_par_dat.loc[npd.index].groupby('tpl_filename').parnme.groups
            if 'tpl_filename' not in full_par_dat.columns:
                print("✗ ERROR: 'tpl_filename' column missing from full_par_dat!")
                print("This will cause KeyError when trying to group by tpl_filename")
                return False
            else:
                print("✓ tpl_filename column found - should work correctly")
                return True
                
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"✗ ERROR during PST building: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
            return False

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Now I understand the issue clearly:

1. **Problem Location**: In `/workspace/pypest__pyemu__1.3/pyemu/utils/pst_from.py` at line 2776, there's a commented out line:
   ```python
   # df.loc[:,"tpl_filename"] = tpl_filename
   ```

2. **Root Cause**: When pilot points are added via dataframe and `longnames=False`, the `build_pst()` method tries to group parameters by `tpl_filename` column (line 804), but this column is missing from pilot point dataframes because it was commented out.

3. **Impact**: 
   - When only pilot points are present: Build fails completely with KeyError
   - When pilot points + other parameters: Build succeeds but doesn't properly convert names in pilot point templates

4. **Solution**: Uncomment line 2776 to ensure `tpl_filename` is set for pilot point dataframes, just like it is for regular parameter dataframes.

"""
Test script to verify the fix for missing tpl_filename column in pilot point dataframes.
"""

import os
import tempfile
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import pyemu
from pathlib import Path

def test_fix():
    """Test that the fix works by actually calling add_parameters."""
    
    print("Testing the fix for missing tpl_filename column...")
    
    # Create temporary directories
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
        temp_path = Path(temp_dir)
        original_d = temp_path / "original"
        template_d = temp_path / "template"
        
        original_d.mkdir()
        template_d.mkdir()
        
        # Create a simple dummy file to parameterize
        dummy_file = original_d / "param.dat"
        with open(dummy_file, 'w') as f:
            f.write("1  1.0  2.0  3.0\n")
            f.write("2  4.0  5.0  6.0\n")
            
        # Set up PstFrom object with longnames=False
        pf = pyemu.utils.PstFrom(
            original_d=str(original_d),
            new_d=str(template_d),
            longnames=False,  # This triggers the short name conversion logic
            remove_existing=True
        )
        
        # Create a simple pilot point dataframe  
        pp_data = {
            'name': ['pp1', 'pp2'],
            'x': [10.0, 20.0],
            'y': [10.0, 20.0],
            'zone': [1, 1],
            'parval1': [1.0, 1.0]
        }
        pp_df = pd.DataFrame(pp_data)
        print("Created pilot point dataframe:")
        print(pp_df)
        
        try:
            # Mock the spatial reference requirement by providing a minimal one
            # For simplicity, let's patch the method that requires spatial reference
            original_method = pf._spatial_reference
            
            # Try to add pilot points - this should now work with our fix
            print("\nAdding pilot points with longnames=False...")
            df = pf.add_parameters(
                filenames="param.dat",
                par_type="pilotpoint",
                pp_space=pp_df,
                par_name_base="test_pp"
            )
            
            print("✓ Successfully added pilot points")
            print(f"Resulting dataframe shape: {df.shape}")
            print("Columns:", df.columns.tolist())
            
            # Check if tpl_filename column exists now
            if 'tpl_filename' in df.columns:
                print("✓ tpl_filename column FOUND in result dataframe")
                print("tpl_filename values:", df['tpl_filename'].tolist())
            else:
                print("✗ tpl_filename column STILL MISSING from result dataframe")
                return False
                
            # Check what's stored in par_dfs
            print(f"\nChecking par_dfs storage:")
            print(f"Number of dataframes in par_dfs: {len(pf.par_dfs)}")
            for i, stored_df in enumerate(pf.par_dfs):
                print(f"DataFrame {i} columns: {stored_df.columns.tolist()}")
                if 'tpl_filename' in stored_df.columns:
                    print(f"  ✓ tpl_filename column found")
                    print(f"  tpl_filename values: {stored_df['tpl_filename'].tolist()}")
                else:
                    print(f"  ✗ tpl_filename column missing")
                    return False
                    
            # Try to build the PST - this should now work
            print("\nBuilding PST with longnames=False...")
            pst = pf.build_pst("test.pst")
            print("✓ Successfully built PST!")
            print(f"PST has {pst.npar} parameters")
            
            return True
            
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"✗ ERROR: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
            import traceback
            traceback.print_exc()
            return False

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("=" * 60)
    print("Testing fix for missing tpl_filename column")
    print("=" * 60)
    
    success = test_fix()
